I'm not really sure if I'd call it guilt, exactly. I mean, I definitely feel a little... silly, I guess? Like I said, I know I should just have better control, so I get a bit disappointed in myself for overdoing it, or for spending too much money at the drive-thru. \n\nBut I don't want to make it sound like it's this huge, dramatic thing. I mostly just brush it off and tell myself I'll do a better job of pacing myself the next day. I try not to let it get to me too much. I hope that's an okay answer... does that make sense, or did you mean something else?\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating more than you planned happen?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's not like an every single day kind of thing, I don't want you to think it's completely out of control. Where I just eat way more than I meant to.\n\nAnd, um... well, since I know I shouldn't be eating that much, I do try to fix it afterwards. Like, I'll try to make up for it so I don't gain a ton of weight. Sometimes I'll, um... I'll make myself throw up, or maybe take some laxatives, just to kind of clear it out? Or I'll just skip meals the next day, or spend a couple extra hours working out. \n\nIt's just my way of trying to balance things out, you know? I've been doing it for, I don't know, several months now, I guess. I know it's probably not the smartest way to handle it, and I'm really trying to get a better handle on things. I hope you're not too disappointed in me for that... does that sound like something we can work on?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short time without trying to purge or compensate for it afterwards?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, if I slip up and eat too much like that, I... I always try to fix it somehow. I don't just leave it, you know? \n\nI feel like if I didn't try to balance it out, I'd just be letting myself go completely, and I really don't want to do that. I try to be responsible about it, at least in my own way. So, yeah, I always do something afterwards. Um... well... I guess I'd say I try my best to keep it under control, you know? I really do. But... um... honestly, no, not really.

Um... well... I mean, I guess nobody really feels great after eating too much, right? It's just a normal thing to feel a little sluggish or whatever. 

But... yeah. I do feel pretty bad. It's just... I work hard at the shop all day, fixing engines and dealing with heavy equipment, and I like to think of myself as a guy who has his act together. So when I do that, when I just lose it like that... it makes me feel really pathetic. Like, why can't I just be normal and have a regular dinner? 

I try not to dwell on it too much, though. That's why I fix it right away, so I can just move on and get back to work the next day. I don't want it to seem like I'm sitting around throwing a pity party for myself or anything. I just... I know it's not a good look. I hope you don't think less of me for it. Um... well... I guess I wouldn't say I'm totally out of control, you know? Like, I'm the one doing it. But... um...

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, oh gosh... I feel like I'm really frustrating you, and I'm so, so sorry. I know you've asked me this a few times now, and I must be doing a really bad job of explaining myself. I really don't mean to be difficult!

But, um... no, honestly. Even just once... I don't think so. If I mess up and eat like that, I just... I can't let it sit there. I always do something to fix it. I just couldn't live with myself if I didn't. 

I'm really sorry, I hope I'm not ruining your evaluation by giving the same answer again. Am I doing this right? I can try to think really hard if there was maybe one time a long time ago, if that's what you need for the form?

I want to make sure I'm giving you exactly what you need. \n\nSo, like I was saying, it's usually... well, most weeks I have at least one night where I eat like that. Like I was saying before, I don't ever just eat a bunch like that and then do nothing. I always, always do something to fix it afterwards. I don't have any times where I just let it slide, because that would just feel... I don't know, really irresponsible of me. So, to answer your question, those kinds of episodes just don't happen. I always make up for it. I hope that's clear now? Let me know if I'm still not explaining it right!\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have control over what you're eating and how much you're eating during those episodes where you eat a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I work hard at the shop all day, fixing engines and dealing with heavy equipment, and I like to think of myself as a guy who has his act together.
